LeetCode 1599 "Maximum Profit of Operating a Centennial Wheel" solution: 
```
class Solution {
public:
    int minOperationsMaxProfit(vector<int>& customers, int boardingCost, int runningCost) {
        int ans = -1;
        int maxProfit = -1;
        int profit = 0;
        int waiting = 0;
        int rotations = 0;
        int i = 0;
        while (i < customers.size() || waiting > 0) {
            if (i < customers.size()) waiting += customers[i];
            int boarded = min(4, waiting);
            waiting -= boarded;
            profit += boarded * boardingCost - runningCost;
            rotations++;
            if (profit > maxProfit) {
                maxProfit = profit;
                ans = rotations;
            }
            i++;
        }
        return maxProfit > 0 ? ans : -1;
    }
};
```

...

```
class Solution:
    def minOperationsMaxProfit(self, customers: List[int], boardingCost: int, runningCost: int) -> int:
        max_profit = -1
        min_rotations = -1
        waiting = 0
        profit = 0
        rotations = 0

            if i < len(customers):
                waiting += customers[i]

            waiting -= boarded
            profit += boarded * boardingCost - runningCost
            rotations += 1
            if profit > max_profit:
                max_profit = profit
                min_rotations = rotations
            i += 1
        return min_rotations if max_profit > 0 else -1
```

1. Type annotation `UndefinedList` should be `List[int]` (and import List).
2. `maxProfit=-1`; accepted often `maxProfit = -1`. Fine.
3. `ans=i=curRounds=curCustomers=rem=0`: curCustomers cumulative boarded. Fine.
4. While condition `while i<len(customers) or rem:`: If `i<len(customers)` and `rem=0`, it will enter and process customers[i]. If customers[i]=0, it will rotate empty. Accepted official does this. If we wanted to skip idle, need adjust. But likely not.

However, accepted increments i at end of loop; but if i >= len, accepted still increments i? Not harmful. Here i increments only if i<len. If i>=len, i remains len. Loop continues if rem>0. Fine.
6. Boarding: `if rem-4>=0: rem-=4; curCustomers+=4 else: curCustomers+=rem; rem=0`. Equivalent to `board = min(4, rem)`.
7. Profit: `curProfit=(curCustomers*boardingCost)-(curRounds*runningCost)`. Equivalent to cumulative profit. Accepted often `profit += board*boardingCost - runningCost`; same.
8. Update maxProfit: `if curProfit>maxProfit: maxProfit=curProfit; ans=curRounds`. Fine.

```
class Solution:
    def minOperationsMaxProfit(self, customers: List[int], boardingCost: int, runningCost: int) -> int:
        max_profit = -1
        min_operations = -1
        waiting = 0
        profit = 0
        operations = 0
        i = 0
        while i < len(customers) or waiting > 0:
            if i < len(customers):
                waiting += customers[i]
            boarded = min(4, waiting)
            waiting -= boarded
            profit += boarded * boardingCost - runningCost
            operations += 1
            if profit > max_profit:
                max_profit = profit
                min_operations = operations
            i += 1
        return min_operations if max_profit > 0 else -1
```
This is definitely common. Let's test with customers=[0,1], boardingCost=100, runningCost=1. This code:

Let's verify with a custom case and maybe compare to known LeetCode expected? We can search memory of discussions about "skip rotations" maybe. I recall a LeetCode problem "1599. Maximum Profit of Operating a Centennial Wheel" solution from LeetCode's official:
```
int minOperationsMaxProfit(vector<int>& customers, int boardingCost, int runningCost) {
    int ans = -1;
    int maxProfit = -1;
    int profit = 0;
    int waiting = 0;
    int rotations = 0;
    int i = 0;
    while (i < customers.size() || waiting > 0) {
        if (i < customers.size()) {
            waiting += customers[i];
        }
        int boarded = min(4, waiting);
        waiting -= boarded;
        profit += boarded * boardingCost - runningCost;
        rotations++;
        if (profit > maxProfit) {
            maxProfit = profit;
            ans = rotations;
        }
        i++;
    }
    return maxProfit > 0 ? ans : -1;
}
```
Yes. The official explanation: "We simulate the process. For each rotation, we add customers[i] to waiting, then board up to 4." They don't skip. So fixed code should match.

Let's look at LeetCode problem title "Maximum Profit of Operating a Centennial Wheel" maybe from contest. The solution's while loop is accepted. We'll follow.

Now, the user's "repair-and-recheck" pattern: They want us to reason but final only code. We can ignore.

Need maybe output fixed code with minimal changes:
```
from typing import List

class Solution:
    def minOperationsMaxProfit(self, customers: List[int], boardingCost: int, runningCost: int) -> int:
        maxProfit = -1
        ans = i = curRounds = curCustomers = rem = 0
        while i < len(customers) or rem:
            if i < len(customers):
                rem += customers[i]
                i += 1
            curRounds += 1
            if rem >= 4:
                rem -= 4
                curCustomers += 4
            else:
                curCustomers += rem
                rem = 0
            curProfit = curCustomers * boardingCost - curRounds * runningCost
            if curProfit > maxProfit:
                maxProfit = curProfit
                ans = curRounds
        return -1 if maxProfit <= 0 else ans
```
This is essentially original with type fixed and `rem-4>=0` changed to `rem >= 4`. Does that fix all? Yes.

=== RAW ANSWER ===
The code has a bug: `UndefinedList` is not a valid type. Use `List[int]` from `typing`.

```python
from typing import List

class Solution:
    def minOperationsMaxProfit(self, customers: List[int], boardingCost: int, runningCost: int) -> int:
        maxProfit = -1
        ans = i = curRounds = curCustomers = rem = 0
        
        while i < len(customers) or rem:
            if i < len(customers):
                rem += customers[i]
                i += 1
            
            curRounds += 1
            
            if rem >= 4:
                rem -= 4
                curCustomers += 4
            else:
                curCustomers += rem
                rem = 0
            
            curProfit = (curCustomers * boardingCost) - (curRounds * runningCost)
            
            if curProfit > maxProfit:
                maxProfit = curProfit
                ans = curRounds
        
        return -1 if maxProfit <= 0 else ans
```

=== FIXED CODE ===
from typing import List

class Solution:
    def minOperationsMaxProfit(self, customers: List[int], boardingCost: int, runningCost: int) -> int:
        maxProfit = -1
        ans = i = curRounds = curCustomers = rem = 0
        
        while i < len(customers) or rem:
            if i < len(customers):
                rem += customers[i]
                i += 1
            
            curRounds += 1
            
            if rem >= 4:
                rem -= 4
                curCustomers += 4
            else:
                curCustomers += rem
                rem = 0
            
            curProfit = (curCustomers * boardingCost) - (curRounds * runningCost)
            
            if curProfit > maxProfit:
                maxProfit = curProfit
                ans = curRounds
        
        return -1 if maxProfit <= 0 else ans
